ParlaMint-AT_2013-06-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00206_d7e4579	Good morning, ladies and gentlemen! I'll open the 206. Meeting of the National Council. [[09:05:30]] Mourning on the occasion of the death of former National Council President Rudolf Pöder
ParlaMint-AT_2013-06-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00206_d7e4604	Ladies and gentlemen, I may ask you to rise from your seats. [[Those present rise from their places.]] Ladies and gentlemen! High house! We want to commemorate Rudolf Pöder, the former President of the National Council. Rudolf Pöder is last Sunday in 89. Year of life has gone from us forever. Our participation applies to his widow, to his family and to all who are close to him in his life. ==References====External links== Born February 25, 1925 in Vienna. He learned the profession of the flight engine mechanic. After the war he entered the service of the city of Vienna. In the early days, he was involved in the trade union and the Social Democratic Party. He first moved to the Vienna Landtag in 1969. In 1975 he was elected president of the union of municipal officials, four years later as vice-president of the Austrian Trade Union Confederation. In 1983, Pöder was praised as a member of the National Council. In his parliamentary work he also proved to be a consistent representative of the interests of workers. He focused on issues of social and family policy, and always represented living social partners. ==References== Rudolf Pöder was elected President of the National Council on 1 February 1989. He was a member of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs until the end of the 5th century. ==References== Even after his departure from active politics, pedders took up the service of the Community. For eight years, as President of the Austrian Pensioners' Association, he represented the interests of the older generation. For his public engagement, Rudolf Pöder has been awarded many awards, including the Great Golden Honorary Sign of Merit for the Republic of Austria and the Honorary Citizenship of the City of Vienna. In his address as President of the National Council, Rudolf Pöder addressed the amendment to the Rules of Procedure of the National Council, which was introduced about the current hour. Since then, it has opened up further opportunities for the honourable Member to debate important issues of the execution of the federal government. The Committee on Petitions was also set up at the time. Powder for this – I quote –: “This new instrument of a better citizen participation will require democratic cooperation, tolerance and expertise from us and all parties involved to achieve the further development of parliamentaryism that we want.” In his opening speech, Rudolf Pöder also formulated his understanding of politics: Tolerance, respect for the opinion of the other and consensus and compromise will not only be a lip commitment for him. Powders continue: 'This means that it must be possible to achieve viable compromises and a high degree of consistency. However, this consensus does not exclude the strength of the mind and the need for majority formation if it proves necessary for the smooth functioning of our parliamentary work." We can understand these words by Rudolf Pöder as the legacy of a convinced parliamentarian and sincere democrat. You can always guide us in our work for Austria and its people. We will always preserve President Rudolf Pöder an honorable memory. [[Those present persist for some time in stupid mourning and then take their places again.]] Thank you. The official protocols of 203. Meeting of 22. ==References== Meeting of 23 and 24. ==References====External links== Meeting of 24. In May 2013, the Parliament's Directorate remained unanswered and undisputed. As prevented, MEPs are reported ing. Hofer, Grosz, Tadler and Muchich. I announce that this meeting will be broadcast live from ORF 2 to 1 p.m. and from ORF III. I also point out that in the next plenary session in the National Council Chamber a photographer on behalf of the Parliamentary Directorate will photograph the homepage. – This for your information. ParlaMint-AT_2013-06-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00206_d7e5307	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen! There are three questions related to the hypo. The first question is: Who put this soup in us? – This is relatively simple: The political responsibility for this, of course, is in the very first line blue and orange, in whatever color, because they have been held responsible for the bank, up to over €20 billion. [[Abg. Petzner: The SPÖ was in Carinthia in the government!]] [[further calls at BZÖ and FPÖ]] This has led the Republic to have no choice but to take over the bank. You should at last be really responsible for this responsibility that you are carrying here! [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] The second question that arises is: How do we get out of this box? That means: How can you spend as little tax money as possible to deal with this bank or at least to handle those parts of the bank that are no longer viable? The bad-bank model, i.e. a bad-bank model, means no automatic loss maximization for the taxpayer. [[Abg. May. Kogler: The opposite!]] We have also applied a similar system to the local credit. Rather, it is about those parts of the bank, of which one knows that they are lost, that one must write them down anyway, even today, or those where there is a risk, where one does not know exactly how this risk develops – it can get better, it can get worse. And those parts that belong to the operating banking business that are healthy that are still necessary for the bank that stay in the bank. The risky parts are taken out into a sort of resolution bank. One part can make a profit in the next few years, the other one will bring losses. That means this concept. At any rate, this has proved successful in local credit, works very well there, and I see no reason why we should not apply this concept in a similar way – of course, because each bank is different – at the mortgage. Mr Kogler is right when he says that if a lot of time is allowed to pass, it will not be better and, of course, it is also necessary to make these decisions and to act here quickly. I personally consider a resolution bank where the risky parts – these are not automatically the ones that bring losses, but those who have a risk today – are removed and the healthy parts, the necessary parts of the bank, sold, used or passed on in any form, at least separated from those risk parts, are a good thing. The third question that arises is: Who pays the bill at the end of the day? That, of course, is also an essential question. When the very first debate took place here in the House, I also made it clear from the Social Democrats that we are not prepared to let the broad bulk be paid by increasing VAT or the same as has happened in many countries of the European Union in order to finance the costs of the crisis. We have clearly said that the financial sector must also pay, and we have also introduced the bank levy, and we have introduced a surcharge. Part of this bank levy would end because it is temporary, and I can say here again: In any event, the costs that come to us justify extending this part of the bank levy, which is limited in time, in order to give a clear signal here that the banking sector, the financial sector has to bear or pay the costs, and that is not just going to be reduced to the broad bulk. As far as the question of who is to pay this bill is concerned, the answer is quite clear, it is to be paid to the banking sector rather than the broad majority. [[Interlocutory calls from Mr Petzner and Mr Mag. Kogler.]] Finally, I can only say that I have the impression that the Minister of Finance here gives her the best to deal with this bank properly. We'll see if it's enough. Thank you.

The project of ecological flood protection – i.e. back dam, plenty of space for floods – has cost 180 million €. And although there was more water, the event was actually stronger, the damage has remained very, very low compared: 25 million euros compared to half a billion euros. Upper Austria, Vorarlberg, many countries have complained that they have not been able to carry out projects to the extent that they actually wanted to do, at the pace they wanted. Six days Permanent rains have led to a situation and water levels, as they have not been observed in the last 200 years. The consequences are, of course, disastrous. Many people have lost their possessions and property, cellars, apartments and houses have been flooded by the water masses, even in many homes the first floor was under water. Ladies and gentlemen! Our solidarity and our support is primarily for the people concerned, the people who have been plagued by this flood disaster. We all have the pictures in front of us. – I was on my way last week in my home district of Schärding. The Schärding district has a special situation: In the west, the Inn, in the east the Danube, and the north of the district bordered by Passau, both rivers flow together and there a level was reached, as it did not exist since 1501, i.e. for 500 years. On Sunday, 2nd. In June, it even washed away the level, so that no more stands could be passed, but which are very essential for the lower part of the Danube. The city of Schärding is particularly affected: The Inn was two meters higher than in 2002 in the so-called high-century water. Half of the city area was under water! And we simply have to note that there will not be a hundred percent flood protection and cannot exist. Thankfully, the first section of flood protection in Schärding was already implemented and implemented at the initiative of the Federal Government and the Upper Austria. Mr Berlakovich, I would like to thank you personally for this. But flood protection has unfortunately not been maintained or done enough because the inn was 1.5 meters higher than the walls that were built there for protection, although these were about 50 centimetres higher than the flood in 2002. Many dams and many protective buildings, ladies and gentlemen, which have been built in recent years, have been kept, and this has prevented damage. 2 billion € have been invested since 2002. And what is the most important thing for the people concerned in such a situation? – Protection and assistance when the water comes, as well as solidarity and support when it is necessary to clean up. This works in Austria at all levels: at the federal level, district level and above all at municipal level. I've been able to convince myself: Professional help by the Blue Light organizations, by the Zig thousands of helpers in the fire brigade, the Red Cross and also the police, a great achievement that has been done here. I would also like to express my thanks and appreciation here, especially in the direction of the helpers. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] It is not natural, ladies and gentlemen, that this kind of assistance is being provided. The population has also provided great support in the area of neighbourhood assistance. People simply came to the firehouses, to the Red Cross offices and asked: How and where can I help? – This is a high degree of solidarity. I would also like to mention the Federal Army. In my district city, in Schärding, 300 recruits were in service. Their use was absolutely necessary, as the water went down very quickly, so that the cleaning work there could be carried out really well and successfully. Austria has come together in this difficult situation – I think you can say that. The voluntary services are unique and great. What to do now, ladies and gentlemen? – The most important thing is to help those affected by rapid and unbureaucratic support, to provide the necessary financial resources – the disaster fund was increased to €95 million, the government has already acted there – but also to help those concerned, for example, to fill out the forms. Many Red Cross departments are currently doing this in Upper Austria. Staff are going to the people in the affected apartments and homes so that these forms can be filled out in in a timely manner in order to be supportive. Time and money donations – which is also a very essential point – are done by the population in a great way so that people can return to their homes and homes quickly. Furthermore, the construction of protective projects must continue consistently. Upper Austria has already announced that an additional €500 million will be invested in the coming years. In the area of the Machland dam, the protective walls have been kept, and of course these projects need to be continued. We must also note, however, that rivers need space. If walls are built in certain places, then it has to be logical to be able to spread the water elsewhere. We need more space for our rivers and streams to spare the apartments and houses. In addition, we need the best possible support for voluntaryness, for the voluntary organizations. However, the measures must not be detrimental to voluntary work. I am very ready to speak on the subject of alignment of workers/employees, if even my own apartment, clean up your own house or must be freed from dirt, but we must be careful that the measures are not exaggerating, so that this does not become a disadvantage for the voluntary helpers. It is now a matter of helping those concerned quickly, above all financially. Austria holds together, no one is let down. Thanks to all those who make this possible!

But I also see it as our task to support them as best as possible here and to provide rapid and unbureaucratic assistance. I would also like to express my great respect and my thanks to all those who have helped, to all those who have shown themselves in solidarity, especially with regard to the performance of the many volunteers. However, I also see it as our task to improve the conditions in this House and to facilitate voluntary assistance in Austria. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] I think it is appropriate, in times like this, to express compassion for this speaker, to signal support. But that alone, I am convinced, is not enough, but as a political leader in Austria, we also have to think about what has gone wrong in recent years and what is now our task; for flood protection is not only current and a topic, but it is our task to take this into account in all political decisions. This is about climate protection and the energy transition, which is about land sealing and in particular about nature conservation. I was also quite shocked that these issues did not occur at all in the speeches of German Chancellors and Vice-Cancellors, and for our government there seems to be no subject at all. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] Climate protection and energy transition: I think this flood disaster clearly shows that climate change has arrived. In many other countries on earth, such disasters are far, much more severe. Unfortunately, if we do not operate quickly and efficiently, we also have to expect ever more severe and ever-increasing extreme weather events in Austria. Unfortunately, it is also true now that we have always said: that the consequences of climate change also come to us economically much, much more expensive than the operation of climate protection. That is why I think it is time to make an active climate change and an energy transition in Austria, because those who say flood protection now have to invest massively in climate protection and energy transition. Unfortunately, the decisions of the last few months have not been taken accordingly. The government has passed an inconsistency-free climate protection law, and an energy efficiency law has not been implemented at all. Nature conservation and land sealing: We now see very clearly how valuable intact natural spaces are that not only Jux or often because of a few frogs of nature are preserved, but that the natural spaces have a very, very important function. In fact, in Austria, 16 football fields are sealed every day. These are huge areas, which can only take up correspondingly little water, and it is our duty here in the house and up to the municipalities – for example, in the case of conversions – to ensure that not only fewer areas are sealed, but also that areas are returned. I could not believe that during the flood disaster, the mayor of Burgenland demanded, for example, that a motorway be further expanded. If you make politics like that, I think you didn't understand what it was all about. I think it is now time for us to think clearly and see that flood and disaster protection plays a role in all our political decisions, that our decisions also have consequences in terms of the frequency and dimension of floods. This flood shows me quite clearly – I hope many others see it now at last – how important environmental policy is, how important is active, ambitious, committed environmental policy. It shows that this is not just about any luxury stories, but about all of our livelihoods. It shows how important it is here to think comprehensively, to see climate protection and energy transition hand in hand with nature conservation, with ecological flood protection. Environmental policy must therefore be seen very, very comprehensively and thought and finally be made again, especially in Austria! I hope that you will all remember the environmental decisions that are still in place, their current speech and floods, and that you will be aware of the consequences of our decisions. For comprehensive environmental protection, Austria needs an independent, strong and committed Ministry of the Environment. Thank you.

And second, along the Danube, various communities were invested in flood protection facilities that proved to have worked, as has been the case in Krems-Stein since 1996. After all, we have had several strands in the education of teachers for centuries, and we are now starting to summarize these strands more closely. ==References====External links== In the 19th century, a university teacher's education, which was dominated by the spirit of time, but also by the needs of those then above all philologically. Since the Reichs Volksschule Act of 1869, there have been teacher education institutions that have developed into the present educational universities. That is why this branchism we find with which we must work, which has strengths on both sides, but also weaknesses. We try to see and use the strengths, we try to minimize the weaknesses or even eliminate them. What are the strengths? What are the weaknesses? – Without a doubt, the strength of the educational universities is their practical context. Most of them teach practitioners, i.e. people who were or were teachers themselves. This practice is, of course, always a restricted one, that of personal experience. In any case, it must be extended by a generally recorded, empirically recorded practice. The weakness of educational universities is their lack of connection to scientific research. This, in turn, is the strength of the universities and actually their core business. This also results in the weakness of university teacher training, which is still there and there. She's gotten smaller, but she's still there. It is the lack of awareness that you train not only scientists – that is, physicists, philologists, whatever – but also teachers. It is a huge difference whether I focus in my education alone on science, its needs and their gain in knowledge, or also on what the educational values of such subjects are, which they can contribute to a school or learning goal. That is, the educational universities lacked science, the universities lacked professional orientation. We fix these two mancos with this law by considering them in the form of cooperation, but also by creating framework conditions. I think that is the most crucial and essential step in the right direction. One side-by-side with these two institutions will be a co-operation where both can bring their respective strengths well. In future, these two institutions will have appropriate scientific expertise and offer appropriate training. So we're going in the right direction. The State cannot issue a curriculum. That's what the institutions have to do. Mr Walser, also in medicine or in legal proceedings, does not leave the State the curriculum, even there do the self-employed curriculum commissions at universities. The state didn't lose anything. The only thing the state has to do is to control the quality. He does that in our case. We do this with a corresponding quality body that we use with this law. Otherwise, professional associations may still do so. We will, of course, do this with appropriate measures from the service provider. Otherwise, I am very much in favour of leaving things where they belong, namely in the autonomy of the institutions. They best know how to create a corresponding curriculum. We only have to set the framework conditions and not give too high a regulatory density. Then it is criticized that the eight-semester Bachelor is not international. It's, of course, international. In his letter, an official of your home wrote quite clearly that sign language is not the mother tongue of deaf children. I believe that we really need to put ourselves together urgently, because there are some misunderstandings about this. The image of the mouth, which I use in the sign language, is German, but it is not subject to German grammar, but to certain rules of sign language. Tove Skutnabb-Kangas, a linguist from Denmark, has clearly defined that there are five possibilities for native languages. Learning the language from the beginning is not possible for deaf children because the environment rarely speaks sign language, but there are four other ways: The language that is best dominated, the language that is most widely used in everyday life, the language that is best understood, and the language in which I can best express myself. For me personally is quite clear, I grew up bilingual, but yet the sign language is the language that is closer to me. I think it is very important if you are concerned that we really put ourselves together in your house so that this right is granted. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] Well, I have resisted a little, but I also hope that at some point we can complete this section so that this right is actually lived, because that is not how it works. Thank you.

It is about combating trafficking in human beings to combat the sexual exploitation of children. However, recommendations from the UN Children's Rights Committee on the Rights of Children, on the sale of children, child prostitution and child pornography are also being implemented. Existing criminal offences are being expanded, systemic threats of criminal offences are adapted to criminal acts against sexual integrity and against self-determination. Punishment threats are also increased. I am glad that the essential provision of psychosocial follow-up to those who have been violated in their sexual integrity is also being implemented. There are more severe adjustments in sexual abuse of a defenseless and psychologically impaired person. However, we believe that criminal law is not the right instrument and that po-grapples can be penalised sufficiently with administrative penalties. Here too, an appropriate penalty is to be found. Overall, this novel of the Sexual Criminal Change Act is a significant improvement, especially to protect our children, to protect people with disabilities, but also to protect them from trafficking in human beings. ParlaMint-AT_2013-06-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00206_d7e14536	Now Mrs. Federal Minister Dr. Karl is speaking. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2013-06-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00206_d7e14557	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! High house! I am pleased that I am here to announce my announcement on the occasion of the 184. A meeting of the National Council and today can present to you the draft Sexual Criminal Change Act 2013, which is also supported by a broad agreement. I therefore consider these amendments necessary, because I believe that the special injustice of serious interference in the legal goods of sexual integrity and freedom of self-determination in view of the special mental injuries caused by them in the current criminal framework is not yet sufficiently taken into account. It was therefore a legitimate concern in the area of sexual criminal law to benefit from the need for the implementation of directives in the fields of trafficking and sexual exploitation in order to increase the threat of crime in the area of rape and qualified sexual abuse on grounds of the classification, but also in line with the increased importance of the legal property of sexual integrity and self-determination. In this proposal, for example, we raise the minimum risk of punishment in the area of rape – there is a doubling of the minimum penalty from six months to one year. There is also an increase in the threat of punishment in the case of qualified sexual necessity – there is an increase in the threat of punishment from one year to ten years to five to fifteen years in the case of death from five to fifteen years to ten to twenty years or in the case of life. In addition, I have also called for a rapid implementation of the resolution adopted unanimously here in the House, which, in the area of sexual abuse of a defenseless or psychologically impaired person, increases the basic penalty from six months to five years on imprisonment from one year to ten years. There is therefore equality with rape here. Thus, a fundamentally uniform picture can be achieved in the event of serious sexual offences. Abuse of sleeping or sleeping-like sexual acts is threatened with imprisonment from one year to ten years, in all three cases: whether it is a necessity or a sexual abuse of a defenseless or psychologically impaired person or the sexual abuse of the unmistakable. On the other hand, in all three cases, a term of imprisonment of six months to five years is now provided for as a basic penalty for abuse by another sexual act. The qualification is now uniform in all cases, i.e. for all forms of abuse, namely imprisonment of five to fifteen years and imprisonment of ten to twenty years or life-long imprisonment. I believe that this will send an important signal towards a stronger consideration of sacrifice-related factors in the context of criminalisation and, at the same time, provide a systematically correct rating in this area. High house! This very important discussion on criminal frameworks and their appropriateness should not be overlooked, however, that this draft also implements important international acts and recommendations and thus corresponds to our mission to provide adequate protection for the weakest members in our society. First of all, the EU directive on the prevention and control of trafficking in human beings adopted in April 2011 should be mentioned here. The requirements of this Directive are already largely fulfilled in Austria in terms of substantive criminal law, because the changes of the StGB in the recent past to the implementation of international standards in the field of trafficking in human beings have already achieved a very high level of protection. Therefore, only more minor amendments to § 104a StGB are required, namely: In the case of adult victims, the basic threat of imprisonment is increased for up to three years at the time of imprisonment from six months to five years; in the case of minor victims, an increase in the penalty is also foreseen for unqualified cases of imprisonment from one year to ten years. Finally, there is also an extension of the crime situation by expressly mentioning the exploitation of beggars and the execution of criminal acts. The further amendments to the StGB are designed to implement, on the one hand, the Directive on the control of sexual abuse and the sexual exploitation of children and child pornography and, on the other, the recommendations of the GRETA Group of experts of the Council of Europe on the implementation of the Council of Europe Convention on the Prevention and control of trafficking in human beings and the recommendations of the UN Children's Rights Committee on the Optional Protocol to the Convention on the Rights of the Child concerning the Sale of Children, Child Prostitution and Child Pornography. Mr Jarolim has also mentioned the po-gross – a subject that has moved us very much in the political debate for some time. However, I would also like to point out the numerous initiatives I have already set in order to protect the weakest in our society in the context of the required criminalisation of physical assaults. With the “weakest in our society”, I mean in particular the children, but also the women, when they are subjected to violence – especially domestic violence. Of course, the aspect of prevention and the strengthening of self-confidence of women plays a very special role. That is why I fully support the Council of Europe Convention on the Prevention and Control of Violence against Women and Domestic Violence, the so-called CAHVIO Convention. It is also being prepared for the ratification of the CAHVIO Convention by the Federal Ministry of Justice and the other relevant departments. In this context, I would also like to point out that neither the various EU equal treatment directives nor the CAHVIO Convention I have referred to call for a criminal penalty in the event of sexual harassment, but it must be noted that the CAHVIO Convention in other areas provides for very far-reaching criminalisation obligations. However, it does not provide for criminal sanctions in the area of sexual harassment. Of course, one must also take into account – it has already been pointed out by some speakers to my expert group “StGB 2015”: [[Intrusion of the Abg. May. Worm]] It will, of course, also be the task of this group of experts to look at this and also to consider the extent to which changes in values in society are still reflected in the current situations and criminal frameworks and whether there is a need for changes here. Finally, on the subject of po-grapples, I would like to take up another aspect which has also been raised by Mrs Anna Franz. In fact, it has pointed out that there are administrative penalties for such offences. In this context, I should like to quote from Mr Jarolim's recently proposed judicial programme. There is no room for criminal standards where the means of civil law or administrative penalties can be used. ParlaMint-AT_2013-06-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00206_d7e16114	Madam President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! I am talking about company law amendment law, including reform of the right to set up limited liability companies. This topic is alive for many years, it has been thought and debated a lot about it. It is by no means such that we have not been fully concerned by the Freedom Party – and I am allowed to add my person – on the part of the Freedom Party. In order to describe the end point of today's voting behaviour, I say, contrary to our rejection of the judicial committee, that we will vote so that it does not mean that one would have opposed political or economic relief. However, approval is given with a certain grim face, because the reform does not deserve the name of reform, regardless of the efforts to be made and to be praised by the competent officials in the Ministry of Justice, which are of an excellent nature as always, in the light of the legal policy in matters of company law. Why is that so? – So far, we have a stock capital of €35 000, which has to be paid in half the cash, i.e. €17 500, in order to establish a limited liability company. The amount of capital required has developed like a sinus curve over the past decades. Up to the 1970 ’ s, it was 100,000 schilling, which would be € 7,500 today. Then there was a big movement, that's what they said: This is far too little, the GmbHs founded with low capital go very often in bankruptcy et cetera. You have to raise this to 500 000 schilling, which is now 35 000 €. The whole was always accompanied by an undisputed, superstitious phenomenon, i.e. the belief that the amount of the stock capital would be meaningful over the amount of capital available to society in the operating process. This is by no means the case. The company equipped with the sufficient or necessary parent capital can consume its entire cash the next day without hindrance. The shareholder or managing director can buy a new BMW et cetera. There is no provision for capital conservation in practical terms. There is no blocked account on which the money would have to lie It is also only possible to get close to a concept of capital conservation, but it is a founding process. The effect is that we have a high number of so-called small companies that become insolvent per year. This is more than 1 000. In many cases, no bankruptcy proceedings cover assets, so that the bankruptcy procedure is not even opened. What does that mean? – This is also followed by a chimary, an existing capital that would provide sufficient creditor protection. The answer is as I have already described it: This is an invention phenomenon that cannot be covered with reality. My proposal was: The founders of a GmbH are obliged to deposit € 7 000, € 8 000 or € 10 000 in the case of an Austrian insurance company with a term of 15 years. This amount shall be kept locked for the benefit of a possible mass manager. Then capital builds up after a year, after five years, after ten years. It would, however, be deprived of the access of the managing director. It would have the effect of a blocked capital and, in the case of insolvency, there would be sufficient money to cover the costs of bankruptcy proceedings and some percent of a quota. That would be a completely new concept. The imagination has not reached to take a completely new path, but one tramples the old path connected with the GmbH old styles and lowers the parent capital. This will not be achieved in the field of philosophy to achieve a capital saving effect with the help of a certain parent capital. This would only be linked to the methodical change I described. Supplementary: If society did not go into bankruptcy after 15 years, the saved capital would be free for society. All those who believe that they are making a start-up easier for young entrepreneurs today should receive the faith. It is of course as always with faith: It means nothing to know. The effect will not occur to God if new ways are not taken by the apologists of the founding support phenomenon, i.e. the Chamber of Commerce et cetera. There would have to be support funds, participation capital, subordinate capital et cetera. That would have to be completely different. Nothing will be solved now, it will be turned a little bit on the screw – please be very, should be. We agree, so to speak, for the sake of good economic peace, because that is what many want. Also in our ranks there are callers in this sense. I bowed, I wish you luck. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2013-06-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00206_d7e16196	Madam President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen. Unlike Mr Fichtenbauer, I am very sure that this is a reform, which is a serious and very necessary reform. It must be borne in mind that the number of business start-ups in Austria has fallen dramatically. Between 2004 and 2010, 1 000 companies were set up less than during the period considered, minus 7 percent. In 2011-2012 alone, there were 361 foundings less than in the previous year. I ask everyone to look at the latest World Bank study. It designates Austria 134. The office of 185 states with regard to the attractiveness of business founders. There is a clear need for action. Why? – Because we all know that the Austrian economy is based on small and medium-sized enterprises, that it is borne by them and therefore depends on their growth and employment. That is to say, we must provide a massive impetus to encourage business founders, with whom we also speed up, reduce and facilitate business start-ups. We achieve this with this Company Law Amendment Act, with which – as I always call it – reform of the GmbH into a customized Founder GmbH. The environment has also changed considerably. Four out of five companies founded in Austria are service companies. It is known, Mr Fichtenbauer, that these need much less capital than a production company. This must be taken into account. Similarly, we must not ignore the changes in the European forms of society which are in fierce competition with our GmbH. On average, a minimum stock capital of € 8,000 is required. In other words, we will be back on the top with the 10 000 € specified. At any rate, we will set a strong impetus that will open the way to more companies and more business start-ups. In addition to reducing the minimum stock capital from €35 000 to €10 000, it is not least the further measures that justify the name ‘reform’. These are the reduction of the annual minimum price from €1,750 to €500 – which is not essential for business founders – and the reduction of the notarial costs of the founding act. And it no longer has to be published in the “Wiener Zeitung”. This will save the company's founder again. [[President Neugebauer shall take over the chairmanship.]] There are now fears – and I admit that one can also see this critically, especially from the academic point of view – that with the reduction of the capital, we are experiencing too much erosion of the protection of creditors. I don't see it like this: I know, above all, from my civil profession practice, that no company – and I agree with you – subsequently manages an expansion with the minimum stock capital. [[in the direction of the Abg. Dr. Fichtenbauer]] It must, of course, build up equity. And the equity subsequently defines its credit quality, which in turn requires it to obtain appropriate financing from banks. The second concern was that all existing GmbHs could now rapidly reduce capital from € 35 000 to € 10 000, from € 17 500 to € 5 000. I think this is also a very theoretical concern, because companies will be aware of the fact that they could, in the end, put a relatively low amount of money at risk or reduce their credit quality. Coming to the end: I am sure that this law is the necessary reform. It will revive the economic location through more business start-ups. But – and I notice that at the end –: Of course, the facilitation and encouragement of foundations is not sufficient. We still need adequate funding for such companies. Under Basel III, banks will probably no longer be able to do this alone. Therefore, supplementary financing to the classical bank loans. Then I call the keyword “crowdfunding” and look sharply in the direction of our koali- Tion's partners. The ÖVP will not let go of it and remain an initiative. This effort is also supported by other political groups. So we should be very, very serious about this, because the expectations for us are high in this regard!

ParlaMint-AT_2013-06-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00206_d7e16512	Next speaker: Mrs Himmelbauer. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2013-06-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00206_d7e16533	Mr President. Mrs. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The aim of this bill is to keep Austrian GmbH attractive to founders, especially in comparison to other EU Member States, but also to strengthen it in the competition of European companies. It has already been mentioned: In the EU average, the minimum stock capital is €8 000. Thus, the Austrian GmbH, in its original version – also compared to Germany and the Netherlands – is far above that. In particular, from the point of view of international competition, a reform is therefore urgently necessary. In addition, a change in Austrian economic life has also taken place – Mr Ikrath has already mentioned it – by already four-fifths of companies operating in the service sector and requiring significantly less capital resources. However, it is also important to make entrepreneurship attractive in itself and to remove obstacles to its creation, especially when it comes to the cost of founding. In this way creative business models are promoted, growth-oriented founders are facilitated access to this form of society and they are not prevented from putting their ideas and concepts into practice. Because it has not yet been addressed: The legal certainty of this form of society remains unchanged. For example, registration into the company book is still necessary. The publication of the annual accounts and the integration of a notary in the establishment of a GmbH are also required by law. Thus, the seriality of this form of society continues to exist. This reform is now taking place in a host of measures implemented over the past few months, starting from the Young Entrepreneur Fund, through investment law to social security for entrepreneurs, in order to ensure that we have a strong economic location that is internationally competitive, but which, above all, provides security for future generations and motivates them to take this step into self-employment. One thing has to be clear: Without these courageous people who face the challenge of creating a company and creating jobs and this prosperity in our society, it is not possible.

ParlaMint-AT_2013-06-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00206_d7e16648	Now Mrs. Federal Minister Dr. Karl is speaking. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2013-06-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00206_d7e16670	Mr President. High house! In the field of company with limited liability, we have seen stagnating figures in recent years as regards the new creations. In addition, with a minimum stock capital of €35 000, we are also very high in European comparison. The minimum stock capital in Europe is approximately €8 000. In some European countries, we see the tendency to choose other forms of society with limited liability. For example, Germany recently approved the creation of a limited liability company even with 1 €. These international trends are, of course, also reflected at national level. Mr Ikrath has already pointed out that about four-fifths of Austrian companies are active in the service sector. Of course, these services companies need less capital than is the case for companies in the manufacturing sector. Of course, such developments at European level, which are also reflected at national level, cannot be completely ignored if we do not want to lose contact with other countries. The Company Law Amendment Act, which you present today, takes into account precisely these developments. We want this law to give impetus to decision-making on entrepreneurship and the creation of new jobs. In particular, this template provides for the following more flexible framework: The minimum stock capital is reduced from currently €35 000 to €10 000. This also reduces the cash to be paid from € 17 500 to € 5 000. This means that the individual will have the opportunity to choose the parent capital in a flexible way for the individual purpose of society and the expected own resources needs. At the same time, however, with the €10 000 a seriality barrier is to be created in order to prevent overcrowded foundations. Another important point is that the costs and taxes, the amount of which is linked to the stock capital, are, of course, reduced. In addition to the corporate tax, the rates for notaries and lawyers are particularly affected. A very favourable tariff has even been agreed for certain simple foundations. In concrete terms, this means a reduction of €1 750 to €500 in the minimum price, and in the case of notary or legal costs, the rate is approximately halved. As has been said, there is also a particularly favourable tariff for simple creations – that is to say, the creation of individual companies. The condition of the Erstein bearer of the GmbH in the company's book is no longer to be published in the “Wiener Zeitung”, but only in the Edicts file. As a result, there is a savings of 150 €. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen, of course this draft has also been criticised. Of course, we have also received critical opinions in the review process. I would say that it is also the essence of a review procedure that one must also deal with critical comments. That's the point of the matter. But overall, the criticism that has been expressed here shows that we have taken a very good medium-sized approach with our proposal. Some people do not go far enough, and they demand a 1-€ society, others say that everything should remain the way it is, it is best not to change anything. The middle path we have taken is therefore, in my opinion, a good one. Finally, in this context and in particular in the context of criticism, I would like to refer to two more points of view. The minimum stock capital does not mean how much capital a company actually needs. I think this is a very important aspect, because the decision on how much capital a company actually needs must be made by every entrepreneur depending on its business model and, of course, by the sector in which it operates. The legislator cannot also take this decision from the entrepreneur. Therefore, even now and in the future, of course, every entrepreneur will have to deal with the question of the capital resources of his company. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen, the citizens also show us in everyday matters that they can take on their own responsibility in such decisions and can also successfully deal with such decisions. For example, everyone who builds a house, or anyone who makes another larger investment, has to deal with what it will cost, how it will be financed, and how much own resources it will require. Mr Fichtenbauer also pointed out that there is no guarantee that this money will actually be available to creditors at the end of the day. It is quite possible and in practice also always happens that this capital has already been exhausted. That is to say, creditors are subject, regardless of the minimum stock capital, to decide in individual cases whether and to what extent they are insured in the case of non-payment of liabilities. The minimum stock capital is therefore primarily to be seen as a serious threshold for the establishment of a liability-restricted society, and this level of seriality has been observed by the present proposal, which makes it 25 percent higher than the European average. The uniform concept for all GmbHs also ensures that those companies with a corresponding lower capital are not liable to the flaw of a low-capital company. By maintaining the rest of the GmbH regime unchanged – I am thinking, for example, of the registration in the company's book with a marked amount of capital, of the establishment and publication of a financial statements or of the inclusion of a notary in the foundation of the company – a very strict transparency is maintained in return for the limitation of liability. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2013-06-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00206_d7e17254	Ladies and gentlemen! This debate includes three laws, we will all agree to three legislative proposals. And the second is, we experience that the best go away after five months, because they don't know if they will actually be taken over as judges with the possible extensions. That is, too short is the time for assessing the potential judges, and part of the best goes away. This is bad, because we need the best in justice, we need the best judges for the country. This is not an analysis that I am doing alone, but I believe that is an analysis, Mrs. Justice Minister, who shares you, because otherwise you would not have tried to correct the course by trying, as early as March 2013, if I did not remember it wrong, with the possibility that every second practitioner will be taken over for seven months, to meet exactly this criticism of this experience. The question is, is that enough? I think we should have an intense debate on whether this was not a save in the wrong place and we should not return to the old model and say: Nine months were a real time. This costs a certain amount of money, but it is worth it for us, because we want the best judges, and we want the lawyers and notaries to be well trained and to have a meaningful experience in the courts. In this sense, I'm looking for a thought process. I believe that our analyses, even if opposition and government sometimes bring different viewpoints, are not so far apart. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2013-06-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00206_d7e17360	Now Mrs. Federal Minister Dr. Karl is speaking. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2013-06-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00206_d7e17381	Mr President. High house! Let me address some aspects of the Arbitration Act 2013. With this Arbitration Act 2013, we are actually setting a milestone. I am convinced that these legal changes mainly benefit Austria's economic and legal location. I also want to explain the background very briefly here. Why companies choose arbitration, so why they submit to an arbitration tribunal, there are very different reasons for this. However, the choice of the place of arbitration is also crucial for the choice of national law at the place of arbitration. This is because it decides on the possibilities for a disputed arbitration. Austrian law currently provides for an appeal over three instances for the proceedings on the annulment of the appeal against arbitration. And in such proceedings, this dispute is not decided in substance, but it is essentially examined whether the arbitral tribunal has infringed fundamental procedural guarantees and whether the arbitration award should therefore be removed. This multi-party move, which is found in Austrian arbitration proceedings, is therefore a significant disadvantage in the competition of the arbitration. Now, if you look at other types of arbitration and look at the jurisdictions in these arbitrations, you can quickly see that the leading arbitration nations have a shorter approach. In the case of very many arbitration nations, a two-position move is planned. For example, there are countries, such as Switzerland, where the legal system only includes one instance. The Arbitration Act now provides the most attractive solution, namely that only one instance, namely the Supreme Court, decides on the abolition of arbitration. This concentration of proceedings is in line with the model established in Switzerland and takes into account the reputation enjoyed by the Supreme Court. We must not forget that the Austrian legal system enjoys a great reputation internationally. As has been said, the disadvantage in the competition of arbitration has so far been for us this three-part party move. If we reduce this to an instance, we will enter into direct competition with the best arbitration nations, and Austria will be able to prove itself very well. If one concentrates on the withdrawal of proceedings before the Supreme Court, it is also appropriate, in my view, to assign to the Supreme Court all other proceedings relating to arbitration, such as cases relating to the formation of the arbitration court. So it's supposed to be. In all, a concentration of the procedures conducted in connection with arbitration procedures is achieved before public courts, which not only involves an acceleration, but also the training of special expertise at central level. I would also like to point out, however, that the existing legal order remains valid for consumers and workers, because it is precisely what the legal groups concerned have called for, on whose request we were very pleased to receive. Ladies and gentlemen! This project is an important step to make the arbitration location and thus the legal location of Austria more attractive. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2013-06-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00206_d7e17531	Mr President. Mrs. Minister! My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! The establishment of the Federal Administrative Court and the new Regional Administrative Courts certainly represents one of the major constitutional reforms of the recent period. In this context, the administrative jurisdiction-adaptability law for the field of justice to be decided today is also necessary. The basic idea of this reform is that each administrative authority should be first and last. According to Article 94(2) of the B-VG, a complaint to the Administrative Court may be brought against the decisions adopted by it as the only appeal, except in very specific cases. The objectives, such as maintaining the shortest possible period of procedure, avoiding additional court disputes, introducing a decisive settlement, are to be implemented by means of this Administrative Jurisdiction Adjustment Act. In budgetary terms, due to the renewal of OGH's competence in matters of professional and disciplinary law, additional requirements are to be expected to be extremely small. However, according to the BMJ estimates, by halving the flat rate fee for interim orders, the amount of revenue will be estimated to be approximately €100 000 per year compared to the previous legal situation. Further losses of € 100 000 a year will be caused by the cancellation of fees in cases of non-violence and non-compliance, however, which may be slightly compensated. However, I would like to say one thing critically – as our Judge Jarolim has already noted in the Committee, and it has also been addressed by Mr Fichtenbauer today – that, in some cases, the national administration courts are planned as the last instance in disciplinary proceedings for lawyers and notaries. We do not consider the whole to be objective, namely, that a national court should be brought in at federal councils. [[Congratulation of the Abg. Dr Jarolim]] We also believe that, in view of the multi-level problem of legal matter, it is possible to avoid a division of competence as far as possible. This partial shift of competence in administrative jurisdiction to the countries has been and will be discussed very critically, and I think that we must take the necessary steps to achieve this. In the next few years, there will be a great deal of attention to developments in this area.

So far, the Austrian Patent Office has been the first and second instance to be in the future be such that the second and third instance is provided for in the ordinary courts. This is undoubtedly a solution in the sense of the Austrian innovative economy. It is important for me to stress that this change does not create any additional costs for the Austrian industry, that those fees which have so far been incurred by the Patent Office should also be paid to the courts. This novelle therefore improves the system of industrial legal protection and represents a truly important and essential contribution to strengthening Austria's innovation and economic location. That is why I would like to thank all those political groups who agree. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2013-06-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00206_d7e19192	Next speaker: Mrs Himmelbauer. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2013-06-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00206_d7e19214	Mr President. Mrs. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen. The area of radio systems and telecommunications facilities is a high-tech sector which is quite quick-lived and also presents a challenge from the point of view of consumer protection. We talk about all kinds of products that affect the radio spectrum; these are door/door openers, these are mobile terminals such as the mobile phone, which is the CB radio, and these are all terminals connected via public telephone networks, such as a modem or a home phone, but not only holistic products, but also accessories and components. [[President Dr. Graf takes over the chairmanship.]] On the one hand, the amendment of the existing law aims to adapt national law to Community law by adopting new definitions and defining more precisely the available instruments of the authorities. On the other hand, administrative changes are made on the basis of the experience gained in order to eliminate identified deficiencies and regulatory deficits of the Telecommunications Authority. The main focus here is on the protection of the end-user, the user. The focus on a national authority and the exchange and cooperation with foreign authorities is intended to make market surveillance more efficient. In addition, the decentralised review will be better coordinated to avoid the currently existing double-tracks. Overall, the improvement of administrative channels leads to a speeding up of administrative action and significant administrative simplification. It also rewords the criminal record of serious violations of the Federal Law and sets out an appropriate criminal framework. Those who fail to comply with an improvement order from the Authority, who do not withdraw a device from the market or who do not recall the product may be punished with a fine of up to € 58 000. With all these improvements and the ways in which they are effectively designed, but also through cooperation with foreign authorities, we will be able to find a solution to this problem in the future. to ensure much better protection of the population against dangerous products.

ParlaMint-AT_2013-06-12-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00206_d7e19460	(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Mrs. Federal Minister! Here again, we have to do with adapting or complying with Austrian legislation to an EU regulation, with two quite legitimate advantages. First of all, the shorter methods of market surveillance already mentioned: This is really exactly what consumers need. If there is an ambiguity when there are difficulties in the market somewhere, then the authority – and that is the claim the citizen claims – simply has to get in the way quickly and make appropriate decisions. These times are over. It is clearly defined by markings, which is in conformity with market and technology, so the market is guaranteed. And vice versa, this means to the consumer, he knows what he can buy in other countries. When it comes to data protection, I dare to doubt whether this would necessarily be taken into account in this novel. On consumer protection, on the other hand, we want, as important as it is and as much as we stand for it as a liberal, but the same, a similar, a uniform procedure. If it is common for the car, for example, that a recall action is made and you drive to the dealer and that is handled accordingly, then we want to do so here as well. In this context, it makes no sense to provide for a consumer protection procedure only in the TKG for the equipment, machinery and equipment concerned there. This may be critical in the first approach, but it ensures homogeneous legislation and homogeneous handling. That is why we agree with this amendment. Thank you.
